New Delhi, the 28th August, 1975 

S.O. 2976. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
3 of the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 (25 
of 1946), the Central Government hereby specifies the follow- 
ing offences as the offences which are to be investigated by 
the Delhi Special Police Establishment, namely; — 

(a) Offences punishable under section 35 of the Gift- 

Tax Act, 1958 (18 of 1958). 

(b) Offences punishable under section 36 of the Wealth- 

Tax Act, 1957 (27 of 1957), 

(c) Attempts, abetments and conspiracies in relation to, 

or in connection with, any offence mentioned in 
clauses (a) and (b) and any other offence committed 
in the course of the same transaction arising out 
of the same facts, 

[No. 228/3/7 5- AVD-II] 
B, C. VANJANI, Under Secy.

ELECTION COMMISSION OF INDIA 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 25th July, 1975 

8 . 0 , 2977 . — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri Nirbhayanath, Village 73 N.P., 67 N.P., Tehsil 
Raisinghnagar, a contesting candidate for General Elections 
to the Rajasthan Legislative Assembly held in 1972 from 
Raisinghnagar constituency has failed to lodge an account of 
his Election expenses as required by the Representation of 
the People Act, 1951 and the Rules made thereunder; 

2. And whereas the said candidate, even after due notices, 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no 
good reason or Justification for the failure ; 

Now therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act. the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Hirbhayanath to be disqualified for being chosen as, and 
for being, a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order. 

[No. RJ-LA/8 /72(58)] 
V. NAGASUBRAMANIAN, Secy.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 22nd July, 1973 

S.O. 2978 Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 

that Shri Nila Das of Village Noalikaibarita, P.O. Noali Miri. 
North Lakhimpur a contesting candidate for general election 
to the Assam Legislative Assembly held in March, 1972 
from 1 12-Naoboicha (SC) constituency, has failed to lodge 
an account of his election expenses at all as required by the 
Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made 
thereunder ; 

And whereas the said candidate, even after due notice, 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission Is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for the failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Nila Das to be disqualified for being chosen as, and 
for being a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

ORDER 


S.O. 2980. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri Munshl Lai, 15/104, Scwa Ka Nagla, Kherla Ka 
Road, Barakhamba, Agra, Uttar Pradesh a contesting candi- 
date for election to the U.P, Legislative Assembly from 
359, Agra West (SC) Assembly Constituency, has failed to 
lodge an account of his election expenses as required by 
the Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules 
made thereunder ; 


2. And whereas, the notice issued to Shri Munshl Lai has 
been received back undelivered as the whereabouts of the 
candidate are not known, and the Election Commission is 
satisfied that he has no good reason or justification for such 
failure ; 


3. Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Munshi Lai to be disqualified for being chosen as and 
for being a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

that Shri Bhaiya Lai, Village Suraundha, P.O. Mau, District

thereunder ;

reason or justification for such failure ;

for being a member of either House of Parliament or of

ORDER 

S.O. 2994.— Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri T, Achyut Menon, Sector I, Bhilai Nagar, District 
Durg who was a contesting candidate for election to the 
Madhya Pradesh Lcgilsative Assembly from 140-Bhilai cons- 
tituency held in March, 1972 has failed to lodge an account 
of his election expenses as required by the Representation 
of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section I0A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
T. Achyut Menon to be disqualified for being chosen as, and 
for being, a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

S.O. 3030.— Whereas the Central Government is of the opi- 
nion that it is expedient and also in the interest of public to publish 
the names and other particulars relating to the assessees, who 
were in default for non-payment of taxes exceeding Rs. 25,000/- 
for a period : — 

(a) exceeding 9 months, but not exceeding 1 year and 3 

monts. 

(b) exceeding 1 year and 3 months, but not exceeding 2 
years and 3 months and

INCOME-TAX 

S.O 3032. --Whereas the Central Government is of the 
opinion that it is necessary and expedient in the public interest to 
publish the names and other particulars hereinafter specified 
relating to persons in default of payment of tax of Rs. 25,000/- 
or more for periods exceeding nine months as on 31-3- 1974. 


And whereas in exercise of the powers conferred by section 
287 of the Income-tax Act (43 of 1961) and all other powers 
enabling them in this behalf the Central Government by its order 
dated 9th June, 1 969 authorised all the Commissioners of Income- 
tax to publish the names, addresses and the amount of tax in 
default relating to assessees within their jurisdiction as at the end 
of financial year 1973-74. 


Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred on me 
by the Central Government by its aforesaid order dated 9th June, 
1969, 1 hereby publish in the Schedule, hereto annexed, the names 
and other particulars of the assessees aforesaid. 


Income Tax Department 

List of defaulters as on 31-3-1974 u/s 287 of the Income-tax 
Act, 1961 (i) is for amount in default for periods exceeding nine 
months but not exceeding one year and three months; (ii) for 
amount in default for periods of one year and three months and 
above but not exceeding two years and three months; (iii) for 
amount in default of two years and three months and above and 
(iv) for total amount in default, 

1. M/s. Banwari Lai Balwant Rai, Sirsa— (ii) Rs. 2860 (iii) 
94878 (iv) Rs. 97738. 

2. Shri Kedar Nath S/o Durga Ram, Village Goad, Tehsil, 
Namaul (iii) Rs. 76563 and (iv) Rs. 76563. 

3. Shri Gopi Chand Dalai, Rohtak — (iii) Rs. 60564 and (iv) 
Rs. 60564. 

4. M/s. Mittar Sain and Sons, Rohtak— (iii) Rs. 31893 and 
(iv) Rs. 31893. 

5. M/s. New Rohtak Bhiwani Transport Co., Rohtak— (iii) 
Rs. 87354 and (iv) Rs. 87354, 

6. M/s. Household and General Mills Ltd. Sonepat — (iii) 
Rs. 1,11,535 and (iv) Rs. 1,11,535. 

7. M/s. Narwana Motor Transport Co. Samana,— (ill) 

Rs. 1,28,100 and (iv) Rs. J ,28,100. 

8. Shri Indcrjit Singh Sawhney, Button Factory Market, 
Sonepat — (iii) Rs. 60841 and (iv) Rs. 60841. 

9. M/s. Sharmik Metal Production Co-operative Society, 
Jagadhri— (iii) Rs. 70910 and (iv) Rs. 70910. 

10. M/s. General Mines and Quarries, Chandimandir, (Distt.

New Delhi, the 13th September, 1975 

S.O. 3034 — In exercise of the powers conferred by Sec- 
tion 7 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) Act, 
1963 (22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby recog- 
nises for a further period of one year the Government of 
Orissa Analytical Laboratories at Jajpur Road (District : 
Cuttack) and Joda (District : Keonjhar), as the agency for 
the inspection of the Minerals and Ores — Group I, specified 
in schedule II annexed to the notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Commerce, No ,S.O. 3152, dated 
the 30th September, 1965.

Office of the Chief Controller of Imports and Export* 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 28th August, 1975 

5.0. 3036. — The Minerals & Metals Trading Corporation 
of India Ltd., were granted licence No, G/T/2412257 dated 
15-11-73 for the import of Refractories & Refractory Mate- 
rial from G.C.A. to the value of Rs. 48,00,000. They have 
requested for the issue of duplicate Custom copy of the 
above licence on the ground that the original customs copy 
of the above licence has been lost by them. It has been fur- 
ther reported by the licensee that the licence was registered 
with Bombay port in India and was utilised partly to the ex- 
tent of Rs. 25.84,467 leaving a balance of Rs, 22,15,533. 



Sec- 3(h)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 13, 1975/BHADRA 22, 1897 3335 


In support of their contention, the applicant has filled an 
affidavit. The undersigned is satisfied that the original custom 
copy of the licence No. G T/2412257 dated 15-11-73 has 
been lost and direct that duplicate Custom copy of the said 
licence should be issued to them. The original custom copy 
of the licence is hereby cancelled. 

The duplicate custom copy of the licence No. G/T/ 
2412257 dnted 15-11-73 Is being issued separately. 

[File No. MMTC/160-161 /73-74/RM Cell/ 1102] 
3C R. SRTNIVASAN, Dy. Chief Controller

Office of the Joint Chief Controller of Imports <Sc Exports 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 1st February, 1975 

8.0. 3037.— M/s. Tak Plastic Works, Jalori-Ka-Bas, 

Jodhpur were granted Import Licence No. P/S/1805355/C, P/ 
S/1805357 /C and P/S/1805359 /C dated 30-3-74 for Rs. 5000 
each for import of Ultra High Makcular High Density Mould- 
ing Powder, Organic Pigment Colour Plasted, Moulding Pow- 
der and Polyster films etc. 

They have applied for issue of duplicate copy of Exchange 
Control copy thereof on the ground that it has been lost mis- 
placed after having been utilised to the extent of Rs. nil 
with unutilised balance of Rs. 5000 In each. 

The applicant have filed an affidavit in support of their 
contention as required under para 318(2) of I.T.C. Hand 
Book of Rules and Procedure, 1972-73. I am satisfied that 
the original Exchange Control Copy has been lost/misplaced. 

In exercise of the powers conferred on me under section 
9(CC) (import Control) order, 1955, dated 7th December, 

72 Gl/75-7 


1955, I order the cancellation of Exchange Control Copies 
of licence Nos. P/S/180J3J5/C, P/S/180J357/C and P/ 

S/1 805359/C dated 30-3-74. 

The applicant is now being issued a duplicate copy of 
Exchange Control Copy of this licence in accordance with 
provisions of para 318(4) of I.T.C. Hand Book of Rules & 
Procedure 1972-73.

CANCELLATION ORDER 
New Delhi, the 23rd April, 1975 

8.0. 3038. — M/s. Rajasthan Brush Industries Dhanetiya 
Lane, Sirohi (Raj.) was granted import licence No. P/S/ 
1800275 dated 31-3-73 for Rs. 4820 from G.C.A. under A.U. 
Category for Import of Raw Material for Mtudcan Fibre, 
They have applied for the issue of duplicate copy (Custom 
copy) of the said licence on the ground that the original copy 
thereof has been lost/misplaccd without having been utilised 
at all. 

The applicant has filed an affidavit in support of the above 
statement as required under para 320 of I.T.C. Hand Book 
of Rules & Procedure 1974-75. I am satisfied that the original 
copy of the said licence has been lost/misplaccd. 

In exercise of the powers conferred on me under Section 
9(U) of Import Control Order, 1955 as amended, I order the 
cancellation of the said original import licence (Custom 

Copy). 

The applicant is now being issued duplicate copy (Custom 
Copy) of the aforesaid import licence in accordance with the 
provision of para 32CK4) of the I.T.C. Hand Book of Rules 
& Procedure 1974-75. 

[File No. P/R-6/A.M. 73/A.V. RAJ & ADHOC/CLA /358)

Office of the Dy. Chief Controller of Imports & Exports 
ORDER 

Bangalore, the 22nd July, 1975 

S.O. 3039.— M/s. Supreme Glass Works, No. 60, D. K, 
Lane, Chickpet Cross, Bangalorc-2A was granted import 
licence No. P/S/1828082/C/XX/48/X/37-38 dated 28-9-73 
for Rs. 5,000 for import of plate glass of sizes above 3 sq. ft. 
as per AM '74 policy. The said applicant has now declared 
the said licence Exchange Control copy has been lost/mis- 
placed after having been utilised to the full extent of 
Rs. 5,000 (face value of the licence), but without utilising 
the facility of Repeat Operation allowed for 1st half of 
AM ’75 period for R6. 2,500 (i.e. 50 per cent of the face value 
of the licence) allowed against the said licence. 

They have, therefore, requested that duplicate copy of the 
same be issued only for the purpose of utilising the facility 
of the Repeat Operation allowed against the said licence for 
the 1st half of AM ’75 to the extent of Rs. 2,500. 

In support of the above contention, the applicant has filed 
an affidavit. T am satisfied that the original Exchange Control 
Purposes copy of the above licence has been lost/misplaced 
and direct that the original Exchange Control Purpose copy of 
the above licence be cancelled. 

[Uo. ITC/SSI/C, 81 /AM-74 /NP] 
R. JAY ARAM NAIDU, Dy. Chief Controller

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM & CHEMICALS 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 18th August, 1975 

S. O. 3046.— Whereas by a notification of the Govt, 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Depart- 
ment of Petroleum) S. O. No. 3423 dated 17-12-74 under sub- 
section (I) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of 
Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Go- 
vernment declared Its intention to acquire the Right of User in 
the lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipelines. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under sub-sec- 
tion Cl) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to the 
Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has after con- 
sidering the said report, decided to acquire the right of user In 
the land specified in the schedule appended to this notification; 

Now therefore in exercise of the Power conferred by sub- 
section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central Govern- 
ment hereby declares that the right of user tn the said lands spe- 
cified in the schedule appended to this notification hereby 
acquired for laying the pipelines; 



3338 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 13, 1975/BHADRA 22, 1897 


And further in exercise of the power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that Section, the Central Government directs that the right 
of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the Central 
Government vest on this date of the publication of this declara- 
tion in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free from all encum- 
brances.

S. O. 3048. — Whereas it appears to the Central 
Government that it is necessary m the public interest that 
for the transport ofpetroleum from d.s. Kosamba GQS-7 (Vil- 
lage Kuvarda) to CTF (Village Pilodra) in Gujarat State, pipe- 
line should be laid by the Oil & Natural Gas Commission; 

2. And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipelines it is necessary to acquire the RIGHT OF USER 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 


3. Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (1) of the section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines 
(Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) 
the Central Government hereby declares its intention to acquire 
the Right of User therein; 

4. Provided that any person interested in the said 
land may, within 21 days from the date of this notification, ob- 
ject to the laying of the pipelines under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construction & 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda-9; 

5. And every person making such [an objection shall 
also state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by a legal practitioner.

New Delhi, the 20th August, 1975 

S.O. 3051, — Whereas by a notification of the 
Govt, of India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals 
(Department of Petroleum) S.O. No. 2729 dated 4-10-74 under 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acqui- 
sition of Right of User in land) Act, 1 962 (50 of 1 962), the Central 
Government declared its intention to acquire the Right of User 
in the lands specified in the schedule appended to that notifica- 
tion for the purpose of laying pipelines. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under sub- 
section(l) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to the 
Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has 
aftor considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this noti- 
fication; 

Now therefore in exercise of the Power conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central Govern- 
ment hereby declares that the right of user in the said lands spe- 
cified in the schedule appended to this notification hereby ac- 
quired for laying the pipelines; 

And further in exercise of the power conferred by sub- 
section (4) of that Section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
the Central Government vest on this date of the publication 
of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission fmc 
from all encumbrances.

S.O. 3063.— -In exercise of the powers conferred by 
Section 3 of the Public Premises (Eviction of Unauthorised 
Occupants) Act, 1971 (40 of 1971), the Central Government 
hereby makes the following amendment in the notification of 
the Government of India in the late Ministry of Works. 
Housing and Supply No. S O. 307, dated the 28th January, 
1959, namely :— 

In the Table below the said notification for the entries 
in columns 1 and 2 against serial No. 26, the following shall 
be substituted, namely : — 

“26 (a) Deputy Salt Commissioner Promises under the 
(Head Quarters), Jaipur. administrative control 
of the Salt Department 
situsted in Rajasthan, 
(b) Deputy Salt Commissioner Premises under the 
Bombay and Madras. administrative control 
of the Salt Department 
situated within the 
local limits of their 
respective jurisdiction

(Department of Civil Supplies & Cooperation) 

New Delhi, the 28th August, 1975 

S.O. 3064. — The Central Government having considered 
in consultation with the Forward Markets Commission, the 
application for renewal of recognition made under Section 5 
of the Forward Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1952 (74 of 1952) 
by the Chamber of Commerce, Hapur, and being satisfied 
that it would be in the interest of the trade and also in 
the public interest so to do, hereby grants, in exercise of 
the powers conferred by Section 6 of the said Act, recog- 
nition to the said Chamber for a further period of one 
year from the 10th August, 1975 upto the 9th August, 
1976 (both days inclusive) in respect of forward contracts in 
gur. 

2, The recognition hereby granted is subject to the condi- 
tion that the $pid Chamber shall comply with such directions 
as may from time to time bo given by the Forward Markets 
Commission.

S.O. 3065. — The Central Government having considered 
in consultation wilh the Forward Markets Commission, the 
application for renewal of recognition made under Section 5 
of the Forward Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1952 (74 of 
1952) by the Indian Exchange Ltd., Amritsar and being satis- 
fied that it would be in the interest of the trade and also 
in the public interest so to do, hereby grants, in exercise 
of the powers conferred by Section 6 of the said Act, re- 
cognition to the said Exchange for a further period of one 
year from the 11th November, 1975 to the 10th November, 
1976 (both days inclusive), in respect of forward contracts 
in cottonseed. 

2. The recognition hereby granted is subject to the con- 
dition that the said Exchange shall comply with such direc- 
tions as may,, from time to time, be given by the Forward 
Markets Commission. 


[F. No, 12(14)-IT/75] 
U. S. RANA, Dy. Secy.

ture, dew point temperature and atmospheric 
humidity. (Price Rs, 5,00). 

This standard specifies the mateiial, constructional, 
performance and other requirements of seed- 
cum-fortilizer drills both animal-driven and 
tractor operated (Price Rs. 5.00). 

This code describes properties of phosphoric acid, 
the nature of hazards associated with it and essen- 
tial information on storage, handling, packing, 
labelling, disposal of waste, cleaning and repair 
of container, selection and training of personnel, 
protective equipmentand first aid. (Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard covors detailed procedure for pre- 
ventive maintenance of agricultural wheeled 
tractor (Price Rs. 6,00). 

This standard lays down the requirements for wood 
and leather marking punches, (Price Rs. 3.00). 

This standard covers general requirements for 
dental airturbine control unit. (Price Rs. 3.00). 

This standard covors detailed procedure for insta- 
llation of agricultural wheeled tractor (Price Rs. 
6 . 00 ). 

This standard specifies methods of measuring volume 
rates of flow of positive-displacement vacuum 
pumps. The pumps covered are those which 
discharge gas against atmospheric pressure and 
which achieve an inlet pressure of less than 1.33 
Pa (I torr) in one stage. The pumps may bo 
with or without bafl'lc(s) or tap(s). (Price Rs, 
5.50). 

This standard prescribes the requirements and the 
methods of test for peptone, microbiological grade. 
(Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard covers Iho requirements for engineers’ 
scrapers. (Prico Rs. 4.00). 

This standard covers the requirements of clevis 
pins. (Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard covers the requirements of clevis pins 
with head. (Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard lays down the requirements for metal 
marking punches (Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard covers the general requirements for 
dental extiaction, for ceps (Price Rs. 4.00). 

This standard lays down methods of tests for round 
bamboos for evaluating the some important cha- 
racteristics. (Price Rs. 6.00). 

This standard prescribes requirements for fabric 
used in the manufacture of radar responsive 
target banner towed behind airciaft for giving 
air to air and ground to air firing piactico in con- 
junction with radar devices. (Prico £R.s. 3.00).

MINISTRY OF HEALTH & FAMILY PLANNING 
(Department of Health) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 2nd September, 1975 

SO. 3075. — Whereas by the Order of Government of 
India in the Ministry of Health and Family Planning (De- 
partment of Health) No. V. 1 1016/4/74/ M PT, dated the 
6th July, 1974, the Central Government has directed that 
the Medical qualification, “Doctor of Medicine” Baylor Uni- 
versity, School of Medicine, Taras, U.S.A, shall be recog- 
nised medical qualification for the purposes of the Indian 
Medical Council Act, 1956 (102 of 1956) ; 


[No. CMD/13:7] 
A.K. GUPTA, Dy, Director General 


Anri whereas Dr. R. G. Burrows who possesses the said 
qualification is for the time being attached to the Makunda 
Christian Medical Agricultural Joint Seva Mandal, Post Office 
Bazaricherra, District Cachar for the purposes of charitable 
work ; 

Now, therefore in pursuance of clause (c) of the proviso 
to sub-section (1) of section 14 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby specifies- — 

(i) a further period upto and inclusive of the 31st De- 
cember, 1975, oi- 

fd) the period during which Dr. R. G. Burrows is at- 
tached to the said Makunda Christian Medical-Agri- 
cultural Joint Seva Mandal, Post Office Bazaricherra, 
District Cachar whichever is shorter, as the period 
to which the medical practice by the aforesaid 
doctor shall be limited. 

[No. V. 11016/19/75-MPT] 
K. C. MISRA, Under Secy.

MINISTRY OF ENERGY 
(Department of Coal) 

New Delhi, the 28th August, 1975 

S- O, 3076, — Whereas by the notification of the 

Government of Lidia in the then Minist.y of 
Steel and Minos (Department of Mines) No. S.O. 
1740 dated the 25th June, 1974, under sub-section (1) of section 
4 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) 
Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the Central Government gave notice of 
Its intention to prospect for coal in 2585.98 acres (approxima- 
tely) or 1046 56 hectares (approximately) of the lands In the 
locality specified in the Schedule appended to that notifica- 
tion. 

And whereas tho Central Government is satisfied that 
coal i 9 obtainable In part of the said lands; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred 
by sub-section (1) of section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acqui- 
sition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the Central 
Government hereby gives notice of its intention to acquire the 
lands measuring 2525.23 acres (approximately, or 1021.96 
hectares (approximately) oescribed in the Schedule appended 
hereto. 

The plans of the area covered by this notification may be 
inspected in tho offico of the Deputy Comissioner, Hazari- 
bagh (Bihar), the Deputy Commissioner, Giridih (Bihar), the 
Deputy Commissioner, Dhanbad (Bihar), or in the office of 
the Coal Controller, 1-Council House Street, Calcutta or in the 
office of the National Coal Development Corporation Limited 
(Revenue Section) Darbhanga House, Ranchi (Bihar). 

The Coal Controller, 1 -Council House Street, Calcutta 
has been appointed by the Central Government as the competent 
authority for the purposes of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisi- 
tion and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) under section 3 
thereof.

MINISTRY OF TOURISM AND CIVIL AVIATION 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 23rd August, 1975 

S.O. 3077.— In exercise of the powers conferred by rule 
160 of the Aircraft Rules, 1937, the Central Government 
hereby exempts, all holders of appropriate Aircraft Main- 
tenance Engineer’s Licences granted or rendered valid by ap- 
propriate authorities of the United Kingdom and Australia, 
from the operation of rule 61 in so far as it relates to 
rules 57, 58 and 60 of the said Rules and directs that the 
holders of such licences may act as Aircraft Maintenance 
Engineers in connection with the repair, overhaul, modifica- 
tion and maintenance of aircraft owned and operated by 
Air-India. 

2. This Older shall be valid for a period of one year 
with effect from the date of Its publication in the Official 
Gazette. 

[F. No. Av. 1 1013 /5/75-A/AR/ 1937(3)/1975]

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 2nd September, 1975 

S.O. 3078. — In pursuance of sub-rule (2) of rule 3 of 
the Aircraft Rules, 1937, the Central Government hereby 
makes the following amendment in the Order of the Gov- 
ernment of India in the Ministry of Tourism and Civil Avia- 
tion No. S.O, 2547, dated the 8th September, 1973, namely : — 

In the said Order, in paragraph 2, for the words "a 
period of two years'’, the words “a period of three 
years" shall be substituted.

SCHEDULE 


MINISTRY OF SHIPPING AND TRANSPORT 
(Transport Wing) 

New Delhi, the 25th August, 1975 

S.O. 3079. — Tn exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (i) and clauses (a), (b), (c) and (d) of sub-sectionf 2) 
of section 44 of the Road Transport Corporations Act, 1950 
(64 of 1950), the Central Government hereby makes the fol- 
lowing rules further to amend the Delhi Transport Corpora- 
tion (Members) Rules, 1973, namely: — 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Delhi Transport Cor- 
poration (Members) Second Amendment Rules, 1975. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their pub- 
lication in the Official Gazette. 

2. In the Delhi Transport Corporation (Members) Rules, 
1973, in rule 3, for sub-clause (i) of clause (d) the following 
shall be substituted, namely : — 

“fi) one shall be representative of the New Delhi, Muni- 
cipal Committee nominated by that Committee from 
among the members thereof’. 

[No. 15-TAG(2)/73] 
N. A. A. NARAYANAN, Under Secy.

MINISTRY OF LABOUR 
ORDER 

New Delhi, tho 26th July, 1975 

S.O, 3086.— Whereas the Central Government is of opi- 
nion that an industrial dispute exists between the employers 


in relation to lilt management of Messrs. Coke Manufactur- 
ing Company, Contractors, Behive Coke ovens, Sijua Colliery 
of Messrs, Tata Iron and Steel Company Limited, Shastri 
Nagar, Post Office Dhanbad, and their workmen in respect 
of the matters specified in the Schedule hereto annexed ; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desir- 
able; to refer the said dispute for adjudication ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) of section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes | Act, 1947 f 14 of 1947), ihe Central Government 
hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal No. 2 , Dhanbad. constituted 
under section 7A of the said Act- 

SCHEDULE 

Whether the action of the management of Messrs. Coke 
Manufacturing Company, Contractors, Behive Coke- 
ovens, Sijua Colliery of Messrs, Tata Iron and 
Steel Company Limited, Shastri Nagar Post Office 
Dhanbad, in refusing employment with effect from 
5th December, 1974, to the undermentioned 77 
workmen is justified 7 If not, to what relief are Lhe 
said workmen entitled and from what date ?

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 30th July, 1975 

S.O. 3087.— Whereas the Central Government is of opi- 
nion that an industrial dispute exists between the employers 
in relation to the management of Messrs. Thapar Intrafor 
Company of India Limited, Contractor, Jamadoba Colliery 
of Messrs. Tata Iron and Steel Company Limited, Post Office 
Jamadoba, District Dhanbad, and their workman in respect 
of the matters specified in the Schedule hereto annexed ; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desirable 
to refer the said dispute for adjudication ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) of section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to ihe Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal No. 2, Dhanbad, constituted 
under section 7A of the said Act. 

SCHEDULE 

Whether the management of Messrs. Thapar Intrafor 
Company of India Limited, Contractor, Jamadoba 
Colliery of Messrs. Tata Iron and Steel Company 
Limited, Post Office Jamadoba, District Dhanbad, is 
justified in dismissing from service Shri Chattar 
Singh, Loading Machine Driver, with effect from 
the 31st December, 1974 ? If not, to whut relief 
is the workman entitled ? 

[No. L-20012/99/75-D. IIT-A.] 
L. K. NARAYANAN, Section Officer (Spl.)

New Delhi, the 28th August, 1975 

S.O. 3090. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal, Delhi in the industrial dispute bet- 
ween the employers in relation to the Central Bank of India 
and their workmen, which was received by the Central 
Government on the 22nd August, 1975. 


BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVT. 
I.T. DELHI 


C.G.I.D. No. 8 of 1974 
BETWEEN 

The management of Central Bank of India. 

AND 

Its workman Shri Yudhishthir. 

Shri Chawla with Shri H. C. Chibhas — for the management. 
Shri Tara Chand with the workman — in person. 


AWARD 

The Central Government, on being satisfied that an 
industrial dispute existed between the aforesaid parties has 
referred the same for adjudication to this Court by its Order 
No. 23/124/69/IRIII dated the 7th September, 1974 with the 
following term of reference : — 


‘'Whether the action of the management of Central Bank 
of India in terminating the services of Shri Yudhish- 
their, Assistant Cashier with effect from the 29th May, 
1969 is justified ? Tf not, to what relief is he 
entitled - 



3388 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 13, 1975/BHADRA 22 , 1897 


2. The case of the workman appears to be that he worked 
for the respondent bank from the 8th July, 1968 to the 
29th May, 1969 though not continuously. It was averred 
by the workman that his services were terminated with effect 
from 29th May, 1969 as he failed in a written test held in 
October 1968 which made him ineligible for any appointment 
in the bank’s service; hence the dispute, 

3. The management raised the preliminary objections that 
the reference was in-competcnt for the reason that it had 
been once rejected by the Government and there was no 
fresh reason or ground for making it; that the reference 
v/as incompetent for the reason, also, that the termination of 
services of the workman was an individual case for which 
reference could be made under Section 2-A of the I.D. Act 
of 1947 and not under Section 10 as had been done in the 
present case; and that the dispute was about the non-absorp- 
tion of the workman in the service of the bank and the 
reference, however, was about the termination of the service 
of the workman, it was, therefore, bad in law. On merits 
the workman's claim was denied in toto. 

4. The workman filed a rejoinder and re-afllrmcd his claim. 

5. On these pleadings the following issues were framed ; — • 
ISSUES ; 

1. Whether the reference is in-competent for the reason 

that it had been once rejected and there was r,o 
fresh reason and ground for making it. 

2. Whether the reference is in-competent for the reason 

given in para 2 of the written statement. 

3. Whether this case is one of non-absorption and not 

termination and is not covered by Section 2-A of 
the I D. Act and therefore it is bad. 

4. As in the term of reference. 

6. In oral evidence the management examined Shri B. K. 
Batra MW 1, and Shri C. L. Chawla MW 2. On the side 
of the workman came Shri I. S. Kao WW 1, Shri M. K. Vcn- 
kateshrawan WW 2, Shri Yudhistir the workman himself 
WW 3 and Shri J. K. Jain WW 4. 

7„ Arguments were heard. My decision is as follows ; — ■ 

8. 1 propose to take up issue No 3 first as it goes to 
the root of the whole case. 

ISSUE NO. 3 ; 

9. The reference as it stands is whether the action of 
the management of the Central Bank of India is justified in 
terminating the services of the workman. Basically, there- 
fore, the dispute relates to the termination of services of 
that there was no termination of the services of the work- 
man. The management only refused to absorb him in the 
services of the bank and to appoint him any further alter 
29-3-1969 as he had failed in the written test held by the 
bank in October 1968 which had made him ineligible 
for appointment in the bank's service. 

10. Now, in order to find out whether it was the case of 
non-absorption or termination, (he basic document would be 
the notice of demand to be given to the management raising 
an industrial dispute. Tt will be from that notice alone that 
it would appear whether what the workman was claiming 
was on the oasis of his dismissal or termination of services. 
It was the notice of demand only which could show that 
there was an industrial dispute between the parties and 
the nature of that dispute. Moreover, in order to give 
competence to the Central Govt, to refer the dispute for 
adjudication there has to be a notice of demand to the 
management raising a proper industrial dispute. Shri Tara 
Chand Glipta who appeared for the workman, however, 
could not show any notice of demand to have been given 
to the management iaising an industrial dispute on the ground 
of termination of the workman’s services with effect from 
29th May, 1969. Some notices were shown and some re- 
presentations made by the workman to various authorities 
were, also, shown by him but they all contained a plea of 
non-absorption of (he workman, on the basis of which the 
workman had been writing lo the management that he should 
be taken back in (he service of the bank even though he 


[Part TT— 

t 


had failed in the written test on the analogy that some other 
workmen had been so employed irrespective of the fact that 
they had, also, failed in the written test. 

11, Thus there being notice of demand in this case raising 
the industrial dispute on the ground of termination of service 
with effect from 29th May, 1969, it is to be found that the, 
case of the workman was one of non-absorption and not of 
termination. It has also to be held that there being no 
notice of demand, the very reference was incompetent and 
the Central Govt, had no competence to make this reference. 
The issue is accordingly decided against the workman. 


12. In view of this decision, the other issues need not be 
decided. The total result is that the very reference is in- 
competent and invalid. An award is made accordingly. 

(Four pages) 

2nd August, 1 97 5- 

D. D. GUPTA, Presiding Officer

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVT

BETWEEN 

t he management of Central Bank; of India, 
AND

AWARD 

The Central Government, on being satisfied that an indus- 
trial dispute existed between the aforesaid parties has referred 
the same for adjudication to this Court by its Order No. 
23/ 124/69 /I Rill dated the 7th September, 1974 with the 
following term of reference : — 

“Whether the action of the management of Central Bank 
of India in terminating the services of Shri Yudhish- 
tir, Assistant Cashier with effect from the 29th 
May, 1969 is justified ? If not, to what relief is 
he entitled ? 

2. The case of the workman appears to be that he worked 
for the respondent bank from the 8th July, 1968 to the 29th 
May, 1969 though not continuously. It was averred by the 
workman that his services were terminated with effect from 
29(h May, 1969 ns he failed in a written test held in October, 
1968 which made him ineligible for any appointment in the 
bank’s service; hence the dispute, 

3. The management raised the preliminary objections that 
the reference was in-competent for the reason that it had 
been once rejected by the Government and there was no 
fresh reason or ground for making it; that the reference 
was in-competent for the reason, also, that the termination of 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 13, 1975/BHADRA 22, IS97 3389 


service® of the workman was an individual case for which 
reference could be made under Section 2-A of the I.D. Act 
of 1947 and not under Section 10 as had been done m 
the present case; and that the dispute was about the non- 
absorption of the workman in the service of the bank and 
the reference, however, was about the termination of the 
services of the workman, it was, therefore, bad in law. On 
merits the workman’s claim was denied in toto, 

4. The workman filed a rejoinder and re-affirmed his claim. 

5. On these pleadings the following issues were framed : — 

ISSUES ; 

1. Whether the reference is in-competent for the reason 

that it had been once rejected and there was no 
fresh reason and ground for making it. 

2. Whether the reference is in-competent for the reason 

given in para 2 of the written statement. 

3. Whether this case is one of non-absorption and not 

termination and is not covered by Section 2-A of 
the l.D. Act and therefore it is bad. 

4. As in the term of reference, 

6. In oral evidence the management examined Shri R. K. 
flatra MW 1, and Shri C. L, Chawla MW 2. On the side 
of the workman came Shri T. S. Rao WW 1, Shri M. K. Ven- 
kateshrawan WW 2, Shri Yudhishtir the workman himself 
WW 3 and Shri J, K. Jain WW 4, 

7. Arguments were heard. My decision is as follows ; — 

8. I propose to take up is^ue No. 3 first as it goes to 
the root of the whole case. 

ISSUE NO. 3 ' 1 * 

9. The reference as it stands is whether the action of 
the management of the Central Bank of India is justified in 
terminating the services of the workman. Basically, there- 
fore, the dispute relates to the termination of services of the 
workman. The defence of the management, however, Is 
that there was no termination of the services of the work- 
man. The management only refused to absorb him in the 
services of the bank and to appoint him any further after 
29-5-1969 as he had failed in the written test held by the 
bank in October 1968 which had made him Ineligible for 
appointment 1 in the bank’s service. 

10. Now, in order to find out whether it was the case of 
non-absorption or termination, the basic document would be 
the notice of demand to be given to the management raising 
an industrial dispute. It will be from that notice alone that 
It would appear wnether what the workman wag claiming 
was on the basis of bis dismissal or termination of services. 
It was the noLice of demand only which could show that 
there was an industrial dispute between the parties and the 
nature of that dispute. Moreover, in order to give com- 
petence to the Central Govt, to refer the dispute for adjudi- 
cation there has to be notice of demand to the management 
raising a proper industrial dispute. Shri Tara Chand Gupta 


who appeared for the workman, however, could not show 
any notice of demand to have been given to the manage- 
ment raising an industrial dispute on the ground of termina- 
tion of the workman’s services with effect from 29th May, 
1969. Some notices were shown and some representations 
made by the workman to various authorities were, also, shown 
by him but they all contained a pica of non-absorption of 
the workman, on the basis of which the workman had been 
writing to the management that he should be taken back in 
the service of the bank even though he had failed in the 
written test on the analogy that some other workmen had 
been so employed irrespective of the fact that they had, also, 
failed in the written test. 


11. Thus there being no notice of demand in this case 
raising the industrial dispute on the ground of termination 
of service with etfect from 29th May, 1969, it is to be found 
that the case of the workman was one of non-absorption nnd 
not of termination. It has also to be held that there being 
no notice of demand, the very reference was incompetent 
and tlie Central Govt, had no competence lo make this 
reference. The issue is accordingly decided against the work- 
man. 


12. In view of this decision, the other issues need not 
be decided. The total result is that the very reference is 
incompetent and invalid. An award is made accordingly.

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVT

Shri S. L. Khanna, regional manager, for the management. 
Shri Madan Mohan with Shri Shiv Singh workman in per- 
son. 


AWARD 

The Central Government on being satisfied that an indus- 
trial dispute existed between the aforesaid parties has referred, 
the same for adjudication to this Tribunal by his Order No. 
E. 17012/1 /75/DTI /A dated the 16th May, 1975 with the 
following term of reference : — 

“Whether the management of the United India Fire and 
General Insurance Co. Ltd. (Unit United India) 
Delhi Region, Delhi is justified in terminating the 
services of Shri Shiv Singh with effect from the 
7th May, 1974. If not, to what relief is the said 
workman entitled. 

2. When the case came up today for hearing before me, 
a memorandum of settlement was jointly filed by Shri S. L. 
Khanna regional manager, on behalf of the management and 
by Shri Madan Mohan on behalf of the workman. Both 
the above-named representatives of the parties verify and ad- 
mit the terms of settlement Annexure ‘A’ and seek an award 
in terms thereof. I, therefore, pass an award in terms of sel- 
tlement Annexure ‘A’ which shall form a part of the award. 


by the General Insurance Employees Association, 44/10, 
Regal Building, Connaught Circus, New Delhi. The indus- 
trial dispute in regard to Shri Shiv Singh was referred to 
conciliation officer (Central) for his intervention. Concilia- 
tion proceedings having failed, the report has since been 
submitted to the Government, At this stage the above dis- 
putes were again negotiated by and between the parties and 
as a result thereof the same have been settled on the following 
terms. 

TERMS OF SETTLEMENT 

1. It is agreed (hat the management shall re-employ to 
Shri Shiv Singh and Sri Kali Charan on and from 1st May, 
1975 as confirmed employee*. 

2. It is agreed that their emoluments on & from 1st May, 
1975 will be fixed on the basis ag if their services had not 
been terminated and they had continued in service, 

3. It is agreed that the period from the date of their origi- 
nal appointment till the date of their re-employment shall 
be treated as period spent on duty for the purpose of Senio- 
rity, Gratuity, promotion, retrenchment and other retirement 
benefits. 

Delhi.

“Whether the demand of Bellary Dist Mine Woikers

And whereas the said management and their workmen have 
by a written agreement in pursuance of the provisions of 
sub-section 111 of Section I0A of (he Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 (14 of 1947) agreed to refer the said dispute to arbi- 
tration of the person mentioned therein and a copy of the 
said arbitration agreement has been forwarded to the Central 
Government ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of the provisions of Sub- 
section (3) of Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 (14 of 1947) the Central Government hereby publishes 
the said arbitration agreement which was received by it on 
the 23rd August, 1975. 

AGREEMENT 

(Under Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947) 
BETWEEN 

Name of the Parties ; 

Representing Employers : 

1. Shri S. S. Marwaha 

Asstt. Personnel Manager. 

2. Shri T, A. Menon, 

Industrial Relations Officer. 

Representing Workmen : 

1. Shri A. K. Ghosh, Asstt. Secy,, 

Donimalai Iron Ore Project Employees Association 

2. Shri Seshagiri Rao, Treasurer. 

3. Shri A. Chatter jee, Exe-Member. 

4. Shri N. Vasudevan Nair, Exe-Member. 

SHORT RECITAL OF THE CASE 

Whereas the Donimalai Iron Ore Project Employees Asso- 
ciation, P.O. Donimalai Township-583118. Dist. Bcllary 
raised certain disputes in relation to the employees employed 
in Donimalai Iron Ore Project and whereas subsequent 
thereto there have been negotiations between the representa- 
tives of the Association and the Management during the 
course of the Conciliation Proceedings before the ALC(C), 
Bellary and whereas the Management and the Association 
arrived at an amicable settlement on 13th July, 1975 on all 
the issues except the one referred to hereafter during conci- 
liation proceedings before the ALC(C) Bellary and whereas 
there was no final settlement on the issue or determination 
of pay scale of Shri S, Shahabuddin, Electrician Gr. II and 
as such both the parties agreed for arbitration vide item 
No. 5 — Demand No. 6 of the Settlement dated 13th July, 
1975. 


TERMS OF REFERENCE 

1. Now it is hereby agreed between the parties to refer 
the following industrial dispute to the arbitration of Shri 
M. R. Rnju, Regional Labom Commissionci (Central), Hydera- 
bad. 


“Whether the demand of the Donimalai Iron Ore 
Project Employees Association for granting scale of 
Rs. 130—160 to Shri S. Shahabuddin, Electrician 
Gr. II with effect from 22-8-69 is justified or not 7 
If so what relief Shri Shahabuddin is entitled for?” 

2. Details of the parties to the dispute including the name 
and addresses of the establishment or undertaking involved.

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion that 
the public interest requires the extension of the said period 
by a further period of six months ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to sub-clause (vl) of clause (n) of section 2 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby declares the said industry to be a public 
utility sei vice for the purposes of the said Act for a further 
period of six months from the 25th September, 1975. 

(No. S-11025/8/74-LR. I/DKIA] 


The Aibitrator shall make his award within the period 
of 6 months or within such further time as is extended by 
mutual agreement between us in writing. In case the award 
is not made within the period afore-mentioned, the reference 
to the Arbitiation shall stand automatically cancelled and we 
shall be fiee to negotiate for fresh arbitration.

S.O. 3095. — Whereas the Central Government, being satis- 
fied that the public interest so required, had declared by a 
notification made in pursuance of the provisions of the 
proviso, to sub-clauhe (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) (being the noti- 
fication of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour, No. S.O. 835 dated the 1st Mai eh, 1975) the lead 
mining indusliy to be a public utility setvice foi the purposes 
of the said Act for a period of six months ftom the 25th 
March, 1975; 


S.O. 3096. — Wheteas the Central Government, being satis- 
fied that the public interest so required, had declared by a 
notification made in pursuance of the provisions of the 
proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), (being the noti- 
fication of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour, No. S.O. 836 dated the 1st March, 1975), the Zinc 
mining industry to be a public utility service for the pur- 
poses of the said Act for a period of six months from the 
25th March, 1975 ; 

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion that 
the public interest requires the extension of the said period 
by a further period of six months ; 

Now, theiefoie, in exejcise of the powers conferred by 
the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of elapse (n) of section 2 of 
the industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby declaies the said industry to be a public 
utility service for the purposes of the said Act for a further 
period of six months from the 25th September, 1975.

SEPTEMBER 13, 1975/BHADRA 22, 1897 3399 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 3rd September, 197J 

S. O. 4015.— Whereas an industrial dispute exists 
between the explorers in relation to Messrs Chogule and 
Company Private Limited, Mormugao Harbour, Goa and their 
workmen represented by the Goa Dock Labour Union Vasco-da- 
Gama. Goa ; 

And whereas, the said employers and their workmen 
have by a written agreement under sub-section (1 ) of section 
10A of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), agree 
to refer the said dispute to arbitration and have forwarded to 
the Central Government under sub-section (3) of section 10A of 
the said Act, a copy of the said arbitration agreement ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section (3) of 
Section 10A of the said Act, the Central Government hereby 
publishes the said agreement which was received by it on the 
14th August, 1975. 

AGREEMENT 

(Under section 10A of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947) 
Between 

Name of parties : 

Representing Employers : Brig. M.R. Apte (Retd.), 

Administrative Manager, 

M/s. Chowgule & Co. Pvt. Ltd., 
Mormugao Harbour, Goa. 

Representing workmen : Shri Mohan Nair, 

General Secretary, 

Goa Dock Labour Union. 
Vasco-da-Gama, Goa. 

It is hereby agreed between the parties to refer the following 
industrial dispute to the arbitration of a Board of Arbitrators 
consisting of — 

(a) Shri E.K. Moidu, 

Presiding Officer, Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-Cum-Labour Court at Calcutta, 

20, British Indian Street, Tst Floor. 

Calcutta-1. 700069.— nominated by the Ministry of 
Labour as the Presiding Officer of the Board, 

(b) Shri C.L. Dudhia, Bar-at-law, Ex-judge (Bombay High 
Court) Vital Sadan, V. Patel Road, Bombay-400004— 
nominatied by the Union, 

(c) Shri V.A. Naik, Retired High Court Judge, frabhat 
Road, Deccan Gymkhana, Poona-4— nominated by 
the management. 

(i) It is further agreed that if the Presiding Officer of the 
Board of Arbitrators considers that any member of the Board 
is not cooperating for speedy disposal of reference, his decision 
on that issue will be final and the remaining members of the Board 
will be fully competent to dispose of the reference. 

(ii) In case of any difference of opinion between the nomi- 
nees of the disputants, ihe Presiding Officer of the Board will 
act as umpire and his award will deemed to be the Award of 
the Board of Arbitrators, 

(iii) The parties further agree not to ask for any adjournment 
and also not to prefer any appeal or to seek (he supervisory 
writ jurisdiction of the supreme court/Judicial Commissioner’s 
Court against the award by the Board of Arbitrators. 

(iv) For expeditious disposal of the reference, the Board of 
Arbitrators will hold sittings in Mormugao preferaby in Mor- 
mugao Port Trust Guest House. 

Specific matters in dispute : 

Whether the dismissal of the following 20 workmen of 
the Mechanical Ore Handling Plant of M/s. Chowgule & Co. 
Pvt. Ltd. is legal and justified ? If not, to what relief are these 
workmen entitled ?
